</div></td>
</tr><tr><td class="sidebar-below" style="border-top:#aaa 1px solid; border-bottom:#aaa 1px solid;">
<img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/96/Symbol_category_class.svg/16px-Symbol_category_class.svg.png" decoding="async" title="Category" width="16" height="16" class="noviewer" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/96/Symbol_category_class.svg/23px-Symbol_category_class.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/96/Symbol_category_class.svg/31px-Symbol_category_class.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="180" data-file-height="185" /> <a href="/wiki/Category:Islamic_State_of_Iraq_and_the_Levant" title="Category:Islamic State of Iraq and the Levant">Category</a></td></tr><tr><td class="sidebar-navbar" style="border-top:1px solid #aaa;"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1129693374"/><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1063604349"/><div class="navbar plainlinks hlist navbar-mini"><ul><li class="nv-view"><a href="/wiki/Template:History_of_the_Islamic_State_of_Iraq_and_the_Levant" title="Template:History of the Islamic State of Iraq and the Levant"><abbr title="View this template">v</abbr></a></li><li class="nv-talk"><a href="/wiki/Template_talk:History_of_the_Islamic_State_of_Iraq_and_the_Levant" title="Template talk:History of the Islamic State of Iraq and the Levant"><abbr title="Discuss this template">t</abbr></a></li><li class="nv-edit"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Template:History_of_the_Islamic_State_of_Iraq_and_the_Levant&amp;action=edit"><abbr title="Edit this template">e</abbr></a></li></ul></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>On 12 February 2015, the <a href="/wiki/Islamic_State_of_Iraq_and_the_Levant" class="mw-redirect" title="Islamic State of Iraq and the Levant">Islamic State of Iraq and the Levant</a> (ISIL) released a report in their online magazine <i><a href="/wiki/Dabiq_(magazine)" title="Dabiq (magazine)">Dabiq</a></i> showing photos of 21 Egyptian <a href="/wiki/Copts_in_Egypt" title="Copts in Egypt">Coptic Christian</a> construction workers that they had kidnapped in the city of <a href="/wiki/Sirte" title="Sirte">Sirte</a>, Libya, and whom they threatened to kill.<sup id="cite_ref-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> The men, who came from different villages in Egypt, 13 of them from Al-Our, <a href="/wiki/Minya_Governorate" title="Minya Governorate">Minya Governorate</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> were kidnapped in Sirte in two separate attacks on 27 December 2014, and in January 2015.<sup id="cite_ref-aljaz_5-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-aljaz-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> This was not the first time that Egyptians in Libya had been the subject of abuse for political reasons, a pattern that goes back to the 1950s.<sup id="cite_ref-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2014, a militia group in eastern Libya declared its affiliation with ISIL and then took over <a href="/wiki/Derna_campaign_(2014%E2%80%9315)" class="mw-redirect" title="Derna campaign (2014–15)">parts of Derna</a> in late 2014. People allied to the group claimed responsibility for attacks across the country, including the <a href="/wiki/2015_Corinthia_Hotel_attack" title="2015 Corinthia Hotel attack">Corinthia Hotel attack</a> in January 2015.<sup id="cite_ref-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-time_8-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-time-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 19 April 2015, ISIL released another video in which they murdered about 30 Ethiopian Christians.<sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Video">Video</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=2015_kidnapping_and_beheading_of_Copts_in_Libya&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Video">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>On 15 February 2015, a five-minute video was published, showing the beheading of the captives on a beach along the southern <a href="/wiki/Mediterranean" class="mw-redirect" title="Mediterranean">Mediterranean</a> coast. A caption in the video called the captives the "People of the Cross, followers of the hostile Egyptian Church".<sup id="cite_ref-aljaz_5-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-aljaz-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> In the video, the leader was dressed in camouflage, while the other terrorists were dressed in black. The victims were all dressed in orange jumpsuits, as in many previous ISIL videos. The leader declared in North-American English:
</p>
<blockquote><p>Oh people, recently you've seen us on the hills of Al-Sham (<i>Greater Syria</i>) and on <a href="/wiki/Dabiq,_Syria" title="Dabiq, Syria">Dabiq</a>'s Plain, <a href="/wiki/Beheading_in_Islamism#Scriptural_justification" class="mw-redirect" title="Beheading in Islamism">chopping off the heads</a> that had been carrying the cross delusion for a long time, filled with spite against Islam and Muslims, and today we... are sending another message: Oh crusaders, safety for you will be only wishes especially when you're fighting us all together, therefore we will fight you all together until the war lays down its burdens and <a href="/wiki/Islamic_eschatology#Isa_(Jesus)" title="Islamic eschatology">Jesus peace be upon him will descend, breaking the cross, killing the swine</a>. The sea you've hidden Sheikh <a href="/wiki/Osama_Bin_Laden#Death" class="mw-redirect" title="Osama Bin Laden">Osama bin Laden</a>'s body in, we swear to Allah we will mix it with your blood.<sup id="cite_ref-Ahram_14-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ahram-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<p>After beheading the hostages, a message appears on the screen: "The filthy blood is just some of what awaits you, in revenge for Camelia and her sisters."<sup id="cite_ref-Ahram_14-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ahram-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> This was referencing <a href="/wiki/Kamilia_Shehata" title="Kamilia Shehata">Camelia Shehata</a>, a Coptic Egyptian woman and wife of a Coptic priest who Islamists believe had converted to Islam and was detained by the Coptic Church because of it. (She later denied the claim.) Finally the speaker declares "We will conquer Rome, by Allah's permission," pointing his knife toward the sea.<sup id="cite_ref-time_8-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-time-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> As in other ISIL videos, the captives wore <a href="/wiki/Prison_uniform" title="Prison uniform">orange jumpsuits</a>, intended as a reference to the attire of prisoners in the <a href="/wiki/Guantanamo_Bay_detention_camp" title="Guantanamo Bay detention camp">Guantanamo Bay detention camp</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-time_8-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-time-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> The group of killers identified itself in the video as the "Tripoli Province" of ISIL.<sup id="cite_ref-time_8-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-time-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> The leader of the squad performing the killings was identified as a Libyan expatriate who goes by the <a href="/wiki/Nom_de_guerre" class="mw-redirect" title="Nom de guerre">nom de guerre</a> Al Qaqa'a Ben Omro.<sup id="cite_ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Coptic Church of Egypt, Egyptian government, as well as the Libyan parliament,<sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> confirmed the deaths.
</p><p>Later, when one of the perpetrators of the operation was arrested, he admitted in the investigation that the slaughter had taken place at the beach opposite Al Mahary Hotel in <a href="/wiki/Sirte" title="Sirte">Sirte</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Aftermath">Aftermath</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=2015_kidnapping_and_beheading_of_Copts_in_Libya&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Aftermath">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>The President of Egypt <a href="/wiki/Abdel_Fattah_el-Sisi" title="Abdel Fattah el-Sisi">Abdel Fattah el-Sisi</a> announced a seven-day period of national mourning and called for an urgent meeting with the country's top security body.<sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> In a televised address, al-Sisi declared his country reserved the right for retaliation.<sup id="cite_ref-time_8-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-time-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> He also reiterated an offer to facilitate Egyptians' evacuation from Libya and imposed a travel ban on citizens to Libya.<sup id="cite_ref-time_8-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-time-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Al-Azhar" class="mw-redirect" title="Al-Azhar">Al-Azhar</a> also condemned the incident.<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> The killings were also addressed particularly by the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council" title="United Nations Security Council">United Nations Security Council</a>, French President <a href="/wiki/Fran%C3%A7ois_Hollande" title="François Hollande">François Hollande</a> and U.S. Secretary of State <a href="/wiki/John_Kerry" title="John Kerry">John Kerry</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> Roman Catholic <a href="/wiki/Pope_Francis" title="Pope Francis">Pope Francis</a> telephoned Coptic <a href="/wiki/Pope_Tawadros_II" class="mw-redirect" title="Pope Tawadros II">Pope Tawadros II</a> to offer his condolences. At an ecumenical meeting with the <a href="/wiki/Moderator_of_the_General_Assembly_of_the_Church_of_Scotland" title="Moderator of the General Assembly of the Church of Scotland">Moderator of the General Assembly of the Church of Scotland</a>, Pope Francis stated "They only said Jesus help me The blood of our Christian brothers is testimony that cries out. Be they Catholic, Orthodox, Copts, Lutherans, it doesn't matter: They're Christian!"<sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_(Hungary)" class="mw-redirect" title="Ministry of Foreign Affairs (Hungary)">Ministry of Foreign Affairs and Trade of Hungary</a> provided financial support of €500 for each families of the victims. <a href="/wiki/P%C3%A9ter_Szijj%C3%A1rt%C3%B3" title="Péter Szijjártó">Péter Szijjártó</a> said "Hungary cannot be a bystander of the continuous attacks against Christian communities in the Middle East".<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> The Obama administration was criticized for referring to the victims simply as <i>Egyptian citizens</i> rather than Christians, the express reason for their murder.<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Egyptian_airstrikes">Egyptian airstrikes</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=2015_kidnapping_and_beheading_of_Copts_in_Libya&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Egyptian airstrikes">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1033289096">.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+link+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}</style><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/February_2015_Egyptian_airstrikes_in_Libya" title="February 2015 Egyptian airstrikes in Libya">February 2015 Egyptian airstrikes in Libya</a></div>
<p>On 16 February, at dawn, Egyptian military <a href="/wiki/2015_Egyptian_military_intervention_in_Libya" class="mw-redirect" title="2015 Egyptian military intervention in Libya">conducted airstrikes on ISIL facilities in Libya</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-aljaz_5-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-aljaz-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> The airstrikes targeted ISIL training locations and weapons stockpiles.<sup id="cite_ref-guard_30-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-guard-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> All military aircraft returned safely to base.<sup id="cite_ref-guard_30-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-guard-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> Libyan air force also conducted strikes in <a href="/wiki/Derna,_Libya" title="Derna, Libya">Derna</a>, occupied by an ISIL affiliate since 2014.<sup id="cite_ref-guard_30-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-guard-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> About 40–50 militants and 7 civilians were reportedly killed.<sup id="cite_ref-guard_30-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-guard-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Canonization">Canonization</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=2015_kidnapping_and_beheading_of_Copts_in_Libya&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Canonization">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>On 21 February 2015, a week after their death, the Pope of the <a href="/wiki/Coptic_Orthodox_Church" title="Coptic Orthodox Church">Coptic Orthodox Church</a>, <a href="/wiki/Pope_Tawadros_II" class="mw-redirect" title="Pope Tawadros II">Pope Tawadros II</a> of Alexandria canonized the 21 Coptic Martyrs as saints. Their feast day would be celebrated on 15 February of the <a href="/wiki/Gregorian_calendar" title="Gregorian calendar">Gregorian calendar</a>. The commemoration falls on the feast of the <a href="/wiki/Presentation_of_Jesus_at_the_Temple" title="Presentation of Jesus at the Temple">Presentation of Jesus at the Temple</a> which is 8th <a href="/wiki/Amshir" class="mw-redirect" title="Amshir">Amshir</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Coptic_calendar" title="Coptic calendar">Coptic calendar</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Vatican_radio_32-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Vatican_radio-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="21st_victim">21st victim</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=2015_kidnapping_and_beheading_of_Copts_in_Libya&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: 21st victim">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>After the beheadings, the Coptic Orthodox church released their names, but there were only 20 names. In the video, the leader's victim was of black African descent, in contrast to the others, who were ethnic Copts. It was later learned that this 21st victim was named Matthew Ayariga and that he was from Ghana. (A few sources say he was from Chad, but most say he was from Ghana.)<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>It is most likely that he was already a Christian, because sources reported that he said "I am a Christian and I am like them".<sup id="cite_ref-copts-united.com_40-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-copts-united.com-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> In October 2020, Christian News Now reported that "Ayariga was a Christian migrant worker from Ghana".<sup id="cite_ref-christiannewsnow.com_41-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-christiannewsnow.com-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup>  In the book <i>The 21: A Journey into the Land of Coptic Martyrs</i>, Martin Mosebach, who traveled to Egypt to meet the families of the martyrs, also states that Ayariga said "I am a Christian".<sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>However, according to some unnamed sources, he was not originally a Christian, but he saw the immense faith of the others, and when the terrorists asked him if he rejected Jesus, he reportedly said, "Their God is my God", knowing that he would be killed.<sup id="cite_ref-21st_Martyr_43-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21st_Martyr-43">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>When the remains of the 21 bodies were found, the bodies of the 20 Egyptians were returned into Egypt except for the body of Matthew Ayariga.<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup> On September 29, 2020, Matthew Ayariga's remains were finally received in Egypt by the new church in Al Our,<sup id="cite_ref-christiannewsnow.com_41-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-christiannewsnow.com-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> the Church of the Martyrs of Faith and Homeland, a shrine built in honor of the 21 martyrs.<sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup> The family members of the other martyrs "expressed their joy at the return of the remains of the martyr [Matthew Ayariga]", saying "Our joy is complete."<sup id="cite_ref-christiannewsnow.com_41-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-christiannewsnow.com-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> Majid Shehata, a daughter of one of the martyrs, said, "It was a surprise to all of us that we see the remains of the martyr Matthew inside the Church of the Martyrs, and this is a long-awaited news and all the families are in great joy and we thank God for having responded to us in the return of the martyr."<sup id="cite_ref-christiannewsnow.com_41-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-christiannewsnow.com-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Return_of_the_remains">Return of the remains</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=2015_kidnapping_and_beheading_of_Copts_in_Libya&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Return of the remains">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>After the expulsion of fighters of the Islamic state organization from the Libyan city of Sirte, government authorities announced it has found a place where the bodies of Coptic martyrs were buried. This was done after the government authorities and the Libyan army arrested one of the terrorists who was present during the slaughter.<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup> The Libyan Attorney General has ordered cooperation with the Egyptian authorities to send DNA samples taken from the families of the martyrs to be compared with DNA samples taken from the remains of the martyrs.<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Later, it was ascertained that the remains belonged to the Coptic martyrs after comparing the samples of the DNA from martyrs families they sent by Egyptian Forensic Medicine Authority with DNA samples taken from the remains by the Libyan Forensic Medicine Commission.<sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 15 May 2018, the remains of the 20 Egyptians (the body of the Ghananian, Matthew Ayariga, was not on the plane), were returned to the cathedral, which was built for them and named after them (<i>Church of martyrs of the faith and homeland</i>) in martyrs village (formerly Al Aour or Al Awar or Al Our <a href="/wiki/Egyptian_Arabic_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Egyptian Arabic language">Egyptian Arabic</a>: <span lang="arz" dir="rtl">قرية العور</span>) in <a href="/wiki/Minya_governorate" class="mw-redirect" title="Minya governorate">Minya governorate</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup> A special shrine has been built in which the remains were laid so that people can visit them. Churches and monasteries' bells were rung all over Egypt when the remains arrived on board a plane coming from <a href="/wiki/Misrata_Airport" title="Misrata Airport">Misrata Airport</a> in Libya to <a href="/wiki/Cairo_International_Airport" title="Cairo International Airport">Cairo International Airport</a>;<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (December 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Celebrations of the return of their remains to the homeland. They were received by Pope Tawadros II of Alexandria and a large number of priests and Ambassador <a href="/w/index.php?title=Nabila_Makram&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Nabila Makram (page does not exist)">Nabila Makram</a>, the Egyptian minister of Immigration and Egyptians Affairs Abroad, and officials of the organs of the state at the airport.<sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup> The Holy Mass in church was presided over by Bishop of Samalout Anba Baphnotius.<sup id="cite_ref-64" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-68" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-68">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>One year later, on 25 July 2019, The Coptic Orthodox Church in Egypt officially requested the Libyan Embassy in Cairo to ship the body of Matthew Ayariga to Egypt. Anba Pavnotios dispatched an official delegation to meet Chargè D'Affaires of the Libyan Embassy in Cairo, Fawzy al-Mabrouk Tantoush.
</p><p>The delegation included the priests of the Cathedral of the Martyrs of the Faith and Homeland in Al-Our, Minya, Egypt, Fr. Marcos Atef and Fr. Epiphanius Yunan, Nevine Ragy, General Manager of Schools and Projects of the Coptic Orthodox Diocese of Samalout and Nader Shukry, member of the board on the Church of the Martyrs of the Faith and Homeland, and coordinator of crisis management for the martyrs families. Hussein Al-Bashir Shafsha, the Libyan Embassy's Social attache, attended the meeting.
</p><p>The Egyptians started with thanking the Libyan authorities for their invaluable cooperation and help in bringing home the bodies of the Coptic martyrs, and stressing that the Pope of the Coptic Orthodox Church Pope Tawadros II of Alexandria, Anba Pachomius, Metropolitan of Beheira and Pentapolis (Eastern Libya), and Anba Pavnotios always prayed for peace in Libya. They handed Mr. Tantoush a letter from Anba Pavnotios requesting that the body of Matthew Ayariga by the Copts, would be brought to Egypt "to be joined with his Coptic brothers in their final resting place". The Metropolitan said the Church would give all the necessary legal pledges and guarantees to honour the rights of all parties should his country ask to have him back.<sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2019, the Libyan government agreed to transfer the body of Matthew Ayariga to Egypt.<sup id="cite_ref-72" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-72">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup>
His remains were finally transferred to Egypt and laid to rest with the other martyrs at the end of September 2020.<sup id="cite_ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup>
</p>

</p><h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Book">Book</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=2015_kidnapping_and_beheading_of_Copts_in_Libya&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Book">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>The lives of the beheaded Copts has been detailed in a book by <a href="/wiki/Martin_Mosebach" title="Martin Mosebach">Martin Mosebach</a> called <i>The 21 – A Journey into the Land of Coptic Martyrs.</i><sup id="cite_ref-77" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-77">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
